# RESEARCH — Jam brands sold in Canada (compiled 26 September 2026)

This file bundles the four research outputs behind script_jam.md. Part 1: standards, labelling rules and legal record. Part 2: shelf grid, prices, label declarations, popularity proxy. Part 3: ownership and corporate claims. Part 4: structure note from the competitor videos that worked. Part 5: viewer comments on the two prior jam videos. Every price: re-verify on shelf on camera.

---

# TASK P1 — Canadian standards, labelling rules and legal record for jam sold in Canada

Research date: all URLs below were opened on **2026-09-26** unless a different date is stated. Tiers: **(a)** primary (regulator, court, company's own page/filing, retailer's own product page); **(b)** named outlet; **(c)** aggregator/blog/SEO/Wikipedia/AI — flagged only, never used as a source; **(d)** documented consumer or media record. House rules observed: composition, common names, ingredient order, origin, ownership, price and legal record only; no health/nutrition commentary. Legal items are labelled allegation / finding / settlement / admission.

## 0. Corrections to the task's own premises (read first)

1. **Jams are in CFCS Volume 10, not Volume 11.** The Canadian Food Compositional Standards (CFCS) table of contents reads: "Volume 10 – Fruits, Vegetables, Their Products and Substitutes … 10.4 Jams, Marmalades, Jellies and Preserves"; "Volume 11 – Prepackaged Water". (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/canadian-food-compositional-standards-0 — page "Date modified: 2025-05-01".
2. **The old FDR section numbers in the task are wrong.** The repealed Division 11 jam sections were: B.11.201 Jam; B.11.202 Jam with Pectin; B.11.203 Apple (or Rhubarb) and (naming the fruit) Jam; B.11.220 Marmalade; B.11.221 Marmalade with Pectin; B.11.222 Pineapple/Fig Marmalade; B.11.223 Pineapple/Fig Marmalade with Pectin; B.11.224 Preserve (Conserve); B.11.240 Jelly; B.11.241 Jelly with Pectin; B.11.260 Boiled Cider. There was no "B.11.209 Mincemeat" or "B.11.210 Fruit Butter" in that run of sections (mincemeat is now CFCS 10.5.3 via SOI Vol 4 s.87; no "fruit butter" standard was found anywhere). Source: archived Justice Laws pages listed in §1.3.
3. **"Preserves" is NOT an unstandardized word in Canada.** "(Naming the fruit or fruits) Preserve (Conserve)" has its own compositional standard (CFCS 10.4.8; formerly FDR B.11.224) and its own prescribed common name (Canadian Standards of Identity Vol. 4, s. 82). It is a different standard from jam, not a synonym. See §2.
4. **B.01.010.1 is the food-allergen provision**, not a percentage-declaration rule. Justice Laws: "B.01.010.1 (1) The following definitions apply in this section and in sections B.01.010.2 to B.01.010.4. food allergen means any protein from any of the following foods…" (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.010.1.html. See §3(vi) for what the rules actually say about percentages.
5. **The "glucose-fructose = high-fructose corn syrup" statement could not be found on any Health Canada or CFIA page.** See §3(iii) and the UNVERIFIED section.

---

## 1. The compositional standards

### 1.1 Current law: how the CFCS binds

- SOR/2024-244, "Regulations Amending Certain Regulations Concerning Food Additives and Compositional Standards, Microbiological Criteria and Methods of Analysis for Food", registered November 29, 2024 (P.C. 2024-1270), published Canada Gazette Part II, Vol. 158, No. 26, **December 18, 2024**. Coming into force (s. 172): "These Regulations come into force on the day on which they are published in the Canada Gazette, Part II." (a) https://gazette.gc.ca/rp-pr/p2/2024/2024-12-18/html/sor-dors244-eng.html
- New FDR definition (SOR/2024-244 s. 2(12)): "Food Compositional Standards Document means the document entitled Canadian Food Compositional Standards, prepared by the Canadian Food Inspection Agency and published on its website, as amended from time to time; (Document sur les normes de composition des aliments)". Same source.
- New "common name" definition (s. 2(3)): "common name, in respect of a food, means (a) the name of the food printed in boldface type, but not in italics, in a section of the Food Compositional Standards Document; (b) the name of the food printed in boldface type, but not in italics, in a section of the Standards of Identity Document, as defined in section 1 of the Safe Food for Canadians Regulations; or (c) in any other case, the name by which it is generally known or a name that is not generic and that describes the food". Same source.
- Repeal/replacement of the jam sections (SOR/2024-244): "72 Sections B.11.201 to B.11.203 of the Regulations are replaced by the following: B.11.201 No person shall sell fruit jam or jam containing rhubarb unless the jam has been obtained by boiling the fruit or rhubarb, as the case may be, with water and a sweetening ingredient." "73 Sections B.11.220 to B.11.224 of the Regulations are replaced by the following: B.11.220 No person shall sell a citrus fruit marmalade unless it has been obtained by boiling the zest or peel, pulp and juice of the citrus fruit with water and a sweetening ingredient. B.11.221 No person shall sell a pineapple marmalade or fig marmalade unless it has been obtained by boiling the pulp and juice of the pineapple or fig, as the case may be, with water and a sweetening ingredient." "74 Sections B.11.240 to B.11.260 of the Regulations are replaced by the following: B.11.240 No person shall sell fruit jelly unless it has been obtained by boiling the fruit, the juice of the fruit or a concentrate of the juice of the fruit with water and a sweetening ingredient." Same source.
- Current FDR B.01.042 (as enacted by SOR/2024-244 s. 13): "B.01.042 If a standard for a food is set out in the Food Compositional Standards Document: (a) the food must contain only the ingredients set out in the standard for the food; (b) the food must contain each ingredient in a quantity within any limits set out in the standard for that ingredient; (c) the food must be labelled and advertised in accordance with any requirements set out in the standard; and (d) the food must comply with any other requirements set out in the standard in relation to the composition, strength, potency, purity, quality or other property of the food." (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.042.html ("Regulations are current to 2026-09-03 and last amended on 2026-06-17").
- CFIA summary page: "moved these requirements from Part B of the FDR to a new document titled Canadian Food Compositional Standards"; "The Regulations came into force on the day on which they were published in the Canada Gazette, Part II"; "No transition period was provided for food compositional standards since there is no change to the regulatory requirements". (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/summary-changes-compositional-standards — Date modified 2025-06-02.
- CFCS preamble: "The Canadian Food Compositional Standards document, also referred to by the defined term Food Compositional Standards document (FCSD), is incorporated by reference into the Food and Drug Regulations (FDR) and sets out the standards for certain foods. These food standards are organized by commodity in 19 volumes." (a) CFCS URL above.

### 1.2 CFCS Volume 10, section 10.4 — VERBATIM (opened 2026-09-26; page Date modified 2025-05-01)

Interpretation (10.0.1): "acid ingredient means (a) citric, malic or tartaric acid, (b) lemon or lime juice, or (c) vinegar. (ingrédient acide)"; "fruit juice has the same meaning as in section B.11.001 of the FDR."; "sweetening ingredient has the same meaning as in section B.11.001 of the FDR."

FDR B.11.001 (current): "sweetening ingredient means sugar, invert sugar, honey, dextrose, glucose or glucose solids or any combination thereof in dry or liquid form. (ingrédient édulcorant)"; "acid ingredient [Repealed, SOR/2024-244, s. 64]". (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.11.001.html

> **10.4 Jams, Marmalades, Jellies and Preserves**
>
> **10.4.1** (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam is the product obtained by processing fruit, fruit pulp, or canned fruit, by boiling to a suitable consistency with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains not less than
> (i) 45% of the named fruit, and
> (ii) 66% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer;
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit; and
> (c) does not contain apple or rhubarb.
>
> **10.4.2** (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam with Pectin is the product obtained by processing fruit, fruit pulp, or canned fruit by boiling to a suitable consistency with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains
> (i) not less than 27% of the named fruit,
> (ii) not less than 66% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer, and
> (iii) pectin or pectinous preparations;
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit; and
> (c) does not contain apple or rhubarb.
>
> **10.4.3** Apple and (naming the fruit or fruits) Jam and Rhubarb and (naming the fruit or fruits) Jam are the products obtained by processing fruit, fruit pulp, or canned fruit by boiling to a suitable consistency with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contain not less than
> (i) 12.5% of the named fruit, except that where the named fruit is strawberry it contains not less than 15% strawberries,
> (ii) 20% apple or rhubarb pulp, and
> (iii) 66% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer; and
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the fruit used in its preparation.
>
> **10.4.4** (Naming the citrus fruit or citrus fruits) Marmalade is the product of jelly-like consistency made from any combination of zest or peel, pulp and juice of the named citrus fruit by boiling with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains not less than 65% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer; and
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named citrus fruit.
>
> **10.4.5** (Naming the citrus fruit or citrus fruits) Marmalade with Pectin is the product of jelly-like consistency made from any combination of zest or peel, pulp, and juice of the named citrus fruit by boiling with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains
> (i) not less than 27% of any combination of zest or peel, pulp and juice of the named citrus fruit,
> (ii) not less than 65% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer, and
> (iii) pectin or pectinous preparation; and
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the citrus fruit used in its preparation.
>
> **10.4.6** Pineapple Marmalade and Fig Marmalade are the products of jelly-like consistency made from the pulp and juice of the named fruit by boiling with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contain not less than
> (i) 45% of the named fruit, and
> (ii) 65% water soluble solids, as estimated by the refractometer; and
> (b) may contain such amounts of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit.
>
> **10.4.7** Pineapple Marmalade with Pectin and Fig Marmalade with Pectin are the products of jelly-like consistency made from the pulp and juice of the named fruit by boiling with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contain
> (i) not less than 27% of the named fruit,
> (ii) not less than 65% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer, and
> (iii) pectin or pectinous preparation; and
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit.
>
> **10.4.8** (Naming the fruit or fruits) Preserve (Conserve) is the product made by processing fruit other than apple or rhubarb with a sweetening ingredient, and contains not less than
> (a) 45 parts by weight of the named fruit for each 55 parts by weight, on the dry basis, of a sweetening ingredient; and
> (b) 60% water-soluble solids, as estimated by the refractometer.
>
> **10.4.9** (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jelly is the gelatinous product, free of seeds and pulp, made from the named fruit, the juice of the named fruit or a concentrate of the juice of the named fruit, which has been boiled with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains not less than 62% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer; and
> (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency of the natural acidity of the named fruit.
>
> **10.4.10** (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jelly with Pectin is the gelatinous product, free of seeds and pulp, made from the named fruit, the juice of the named fruit or a concentrate of the juice of the named fruit, which has been boiled with water and a sweetening ingredient;
> (a) contains
> (i) not less than the equivalent of 32% juice of the named fruit,
> (ii) not less than 62% water soluble solids, as estimated by the refractometer, and
> (iii) pectin or pectinous preparation; and
> (b) may contain
> (i) such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit, and
> (ii) juice of another fruit.
>
> **10.4.11** The standards set out in this Volume for jam and jelly do not apply to cranberry sauce, jellied cranberry, cranberry jelly, mint jelly and jellied mint.

Observation (not a legal conclusion): the old B.11.201(c)(i) expressly allowed "such amount of added pectin … as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural pectin content" in plain Jam; CFCS 10.4.1 lists only an acid ingredient. The permitted-additive clauses (Class II preservative, pH adjusting agent, antifoaming agent, food colour) no longer appear in the standard text; whether they remain permitted is governed by Health Canada's Lists of Permitted Food Additives, which were not checked for this task (see UNVERIFIED).

### 1.4 Canadian Standards of Identity, Volume 4 (SFCR-incorporated) — common names, VERBATIM (a)

https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/canadian-standards-identity-volume-4 — Date modified 2024-12-18.

- Interpretation: ""sweetening ingredient" means white sugar, brown sugar, yellow sugar, golden sugar, liquid sugar, invert sugar, honey, cane sugar, maple sugar, maple syrup, molasses, refined sugar syrup, refiner's syrup, golden syrup, corn syrup, glucose, dextrose, fructose or any combination of those substances in dry or liquid form. (ingrédient édulcorant)" (Note: broader than FDR B.11.001's list.)
- "Jam 74. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.1 of Volume 10 of the FCSD, except that it (a) must not be obtained by processing fruit pulp that is preserved in sulphur dioxide; and (b) may contain a sweetening ingredient and concentrated juice."
- "75. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam with Pectin is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.2 of Volume 10 of the FCSD except that it may contain a sweetening ingredient."
- "76. Apple and (naming the fruit or fruits) Jam and Rhubarb and (naming the fruit or fruits) Jam are the foods that meet the standard set out in section 10.4.3 of Volume 10 of the FCSD except that it may contain a sweetening ingredient."
- "Jelly 77. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jelly is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.9 of Volume 10 of the FCSD, except that it (a) must not be obtained by processing fruit juice or concentrated fruit juice that contains sulphurous acid; and (b) may contain a preservative, a sweetening ingredient and concentrated juice."
- "78. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jelly with Pectin is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.10 of Volume 10 of the FCSD except that it may contain a sweetening ingredient."
- "79. The standards set out in sections 77 and 78 do not apply to cranberry jelly or jellied cranberries."
- "Marmalade 80. (Naming the citrus fruit or citrus fruits) Marmalade is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.4 of Volume 10 of the FCSD, except that it may contain a preservative, a sweetening ingredient and concentrated juice."
- "81. (Naming the citrus fruit or citrus fruits) Marmalade with Pectin is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.5 of Volume 10 of the FCSD except that it may contain a sweetening ingredient."
- "82. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Preserve or (Naming the fruit or fruits) Conserve is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.8 of Volume 10 of the FCSD, except that it may be processed with water and contains a sweetening ingredient."
- "Cranberries 83. Cranberry Jelly or Jellied Cranberries … must contain at least 40% soluble solids…" "Mincemeat 87. Mincemeat, Mince Meat, Mince or Fruit Mince is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.5.3 of Volume 10 of the FCSD." "Pie Filler 88. Pie Filler or Pie Filling … must contain at least 30% soluble solids – or, in the case of apple or pumpkin pie filler or pie filling, at least 20% soluble solids…"

CFIA's processed-fruit labelling page confirms the names are prescribed: "The common name must appear on the principal display panel of prepackaged processed fruit or vegetable products [B.01.006(1), FDR; 218(1)(a), SFCR]." and its table lists "(Naming the fruit or fruits) jam | 74", "(Naming the fruit or fruits) jam with pectin | 75", "…jelly | 77", "…jelly with pectin | 78", "(Naming the citrus fruit or citrus fruits) marmalade | 80", "…marmalade with pectin | 81", "(Naming the fruit or fruits) preserve, (naming the fruit or fruits) conserve | 82", "Mincemeat, mince meat, mince, fruit mince | 87". (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/processed-fruit-vegetable-products — Date modified 2024-03-18. FDR B.01.006(1): "The common name of the food shall be shown on the principal display panel." (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.006.html

### 1.5 The numbers the script can say (all confirmed by both the current CFCS text and the pre-2024 FDR text)

| Common name (Canada) | Minimum named fruit | Minimum water-soluble solids (refractometer) | Pectin |
|---|---|---|---|
| (Fruit) Jam | 45% | 66% | not listed in CFCS 10.4.1 (old FDR allowed added pectin only to compensate a deficiency) |
| (Fruit) Jam with Pectin | 27% | 66% | must contain pectin or pectinous preparation |
| Apple/Rhubarb and (Fruit) Jam | 12.5% named fruit (15% if strawberry) + 20% apple or rhubarb pulp | 66% | permitted (old FDR) |
| (Citrus) Marmalade | no minimum | 65% | none listed |
| (Citrus) Marmalade with Pectin | 27% (peel/pulp/juice combined) | 65% | required |
| Pineapple / Fig Marmalade | 45% | 65% | none listed |
| Pineapple / Fig Marmalade with Pectin | 27% | 65% | required |
| (Fruit) Preserve / Conserve | 45 parts fruit per 55 parts sweetening ingredient (dry basis) | 60% | none listed |
| (Fruit) Jelly | no minimum | 62% | not listed in CFCS |
| (Fruit) Jelly with Pectin | equivalent of 32% juice | 62% | required |

Jam and Jam with Pectin "does not contain apple or rhubarb"; the jam/jelly standards "do not apply to cranberry sauce, jellied cranberry, cranberry jelly, mint jelly and jellied mint" (10.4.11). The "45% fruit / 66% soluble solids" and "27% / 66%" figures in the task are correct.

Company restatement of the same figures (a): Smucker Foods of Canada FAQ, https://www.smuckers.ca/en/faq — "PURE JAM is made from a blend of fruit and fruit puree. It has a minimum fruit content of 45% and a (brix) sweetness level of 66%. It has no preservatives, no added colour and no added flavour."; "JAM WITH PECTIN is made from a blend of fruit and fruit puree. It has a minimum fruit content of 27% and a minimum (brix) sweetness level of 66%. It may contain pectin, preservatives, and colour."; "JELLY is made from fruit juice or a concentrate of the juice. Jelly is clear and firm enough to hold its shape when turned out of its container. It has a minimum (brix) sweetness level of 62%"; footer "© / TM / ® Smucker Foods of Canada Corp. or its affiliates."

## 2. Words with no standard

Method: full-text search of the CFCS page (all 19 volumes), the SOI Volume 4 page, and the CFIA "Labelling requirements for processed fruit or vegetable products" page (Date modified 2024-03-18) for the strings "spread", "fruit spread", "pure", "traditional", "homestyle", "double fruit", "triple fruit". Results: **zero occurrences of "spread" / "fruit spread"** on any of the three pages; no standard or definition of "double fruit", "triple fruit", "traditional" or "homestyle" exists in the CFCS or SOI.

- **"Fruit spread" / "spread"** — no compositional standard, no prescribed common name in Canada. Only industry statement found (a): Smucker Foods of Canada FAQ (URL above): "FRUIT SPREADS there are no requirements for (brix) sweetness or fruit content in fruit spreads. A fruit spread is a product that is not a jam, jelly or marmalade."
- **"Preserves"** — standardized (see §1.2 10.4.8 and §1.4 s. 82). CFIA's processed-fruit table lists "(Naming the fruit or fruits) preserve, (naming the fruit or fruits) conserve | 82" as a separate prescribed common name from jam (s. 74). Nowhere in the CFCS, SOI Vol. 4 or the CFIA processed-fruit page is "preserve" described as a synonym of "jam"; in Canada it is a distinct standard (45:55 fruit-to-sweetener by weight on a dry basis; 60% soluble solids) versus jam (45% fruit; 66% soluble solids). Note for the script: Bonne Maman sells its products as "Preserves" in the US (bonnemaman.us) but its Canadian site lists "Strawberry Jam", "Raspberry Jam", "Apricot Jam", "4 Fruit Jam", "Strawberry and Wild Strawberry Spread", "Mango and Peach Spread", etc. (a) https://bonnemaman.ca/en/collections/jams
- **"Pure" / "100% pure"** — no compositional standard; CFIA guidance (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/composition-and-quality (Date modified 2025-01-15): "The term "pure" should not be used on the labels of, or in connection with, an article of food that is a compound, mixture, imitation or substitute. Consumers expect a food described as "pure" or "100% pure" to be uncontaminated and unadulterated, and to contain only substances or ingredients that are understood to be part of the food so described." … "Therefore, the claim is not appropriate on a food that contains any preservatives, antifoaming agents or colour even though the standards may permit them. In some cases, this claim is considered to be synonymous with the claim "contains no preservatives"." … "In a few cases, however, it may be possible to describe a standardized multi-ingredient food as "pure" on condition that: none of the optional ingredients permitted by that standard are added to the food, and the common name allowed and used to describe the food includes the names of all the ingredients of the food". (Relevant to "Kraft PURE" and "Smucker's Pure" jam branding; CFIA guidance, not a finding about any brand.)
- **"Homestyle" / "homemade"** — CFIA method-of-production page (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/method-production-claims (Date modified 2024-01-24): "The term "homemade" describes a food that is not commercially prepared. "Homemade" foods do not require further preparation. The use of a brand name or trademark symbol in conjunction with the term "homemade" is considered misleading if the food is prepared in a commercial establishment, including small, artisan like establishments." "The terms "homemade style", "home-style", "like homemade" may be used to describe a food that may contain mixes, in whole or in part, from commercial or private recipes." No compositional standard.
- **"Traditional"** — no CFIA definition found; the only CFIA use is descriptive: "The claim "artisan made" is not specific for a particular type of food, rather it is a manufacturing method that would be considered traditional and rudimentary, involving a significant portion of manual labour and a limited use of automated machines…" (same page). No standard.
- **"Double fruit" / "triple fruit"** — nothing in CFCS/SOI/CFIA pages. "Double Fruit" appears as a Smucker trademark: J.M. Smucker Co. Form 10-K for fiscal 2004: "Major trademarks primarily utilized in the special markets include: Smucker's, R. W. Knudsen Family, Simply Nutritious, Recharge, Santa Cruz Organic, Spritzer, and Double Fruit." (a) https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/91419/000095015204005304/l08405ae10vk.htm. "TRIPLE FRUITS®" is used by E.D. Smith as a registered mark on fruit spreads (product URLs https://edsmith.com/products/triple-fruits/ and https://edsmith.com/edsmith_product/triple-fruits-strawberry-raspberry-plum/ were returned by search but the site returned HTTP 503 / no connection on 2026-09-26 — content not read).

## 3. Ingredient list rules relevant to jam

### (i) Descending order by weight — FDR B.01.008.2(3) (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.008.2.html ("current to 2026-09-03")

"(3) In a list of ingredients, ingredients shall be shown (a) in descending order of their proportion by weight of the prepackaged product, determined before they are combined to form the prepackaged product; (b) in lower case letters, except that upper case letters shall be used to show (i) the first letter of each ingredient…; and (c) separated by a bullet point or a comma. (4) Despite paragraph (3)(a), the following ingredients may be shown at the end of the list of ingredients, in any order: (a) spices, herbs and other seasonings…; (b) natural flavours and artificial flavours; (c) flavour enhancers; (d) food additives, except ingredients of food additive preparations…; (e) vitamins; (f) salts or derivatives of vitamins; (g) mineral nutrients; (h) salts of mineral nutrients; and (i) supplemental ingredients." Amendment history on the page: "SOR/2016-305, s. 5 | SOR/2021-46, s. 2 | SOR/2022-143, s. 6 | SOR/2022-168, s. 3 | SOR/2022-169, s. 5 | SOR/2022-197, s. 3 | SOR/2024-244, s. 7 | SOR/2026-113, s. 15(F)". (The task's "B.01.008" is the parent section; the order rule sits in B.01.008.2(3)(a).)

CFIA explanation (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/list-ingredients-and-allergens (Date modified 2026-02-11): "Ingredients must be declared by their common name in descending order of their proportion by weight of the prepackaged product. The order must be the order of the ingredients before they are combined to form the prepackaged product. In other words, based on what was added to the mixing bowl [B.01.008.2(3)(a), FDR]."

Note on food additives in jam (e.g., citric acid, pectin, dimethylpolysiloxane): under B.01.008.2(4)(d) food additives "may be shown at the end of the list of ingredients, in any order", so their position does not indicate weight.

### (ii) The sugars-grouping rule — FDR B.01.008.3 VERBATIM (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.008.3.html

"B.01.008.3 (1) If a prepackaged product contains one or more sugars-based ingredients, despite the order of presentation referred to in paragraph B.01.008.2(3)(a), the sugars-based ingredient or ingredients shall be shown in the list of ingredients, in parentheses, immediately following the term (a) "Sugars" in the English version of the list; and (b) "Sucres" in the French version of the list.
(2) The term "Sugars" referred to in subsection (1) shall be shown in the list of ingredients (a) in descending order of the proportion by weight of all the sugars-based ingredients in the prepackaged product, before they are combined to form the product; and (b) separated from other ingredients by a bullet point or a comma.
(3) Each sugars-based ingredient mentioned immediately following the term "Sugars" shall be shown, (a) despite paragraph B.01.008.2(3)(b), entirely in lower case letters; and (b) in the case of more than one sugars-based ingredient, (i) in descending order of its proportion by weight of the prepackaged product as prescribed by subsection B.01.008.2(3), and (ii) separated by a comma.
(4) Subsections (1) to (3) do not apply to the following prepackaged products: (a) a sweetening agent; (b) fruit or vegetable juice or vegetable drink that does not contain any sweetening agent…; (c) fruit or vegetable purée that does not contain any sweetening agent as well as any mixture of those purées; (d) prepackaged products that contain only one sugars-based ingredient that contains the word "sugar" in its common name; (e) formulated liquid diets, human milk fortifiers and human milk substitutes; and (f) prepackaged products that contain less than 0.5 g of sugars per serving of stated size.
SOR/2016-305, s. 5; SOR/2021-57, s. 5; SOR/2022-143, s. 7; SOR/2022-197, s. 4"

Origin of the rule: SOR/2016-305, "Regulations Amending the Food and Drug Regulations (Nutrition Labelling, Other Labelling Provisions and Food Colours)", Canada Gazette Part II, 2016-12-14, s. 5 enacted B.01.008.3. (a) https://gazette.gc.ca/rp-pr/p2/2016/2016-12-14/html/sor-dors305-eng.html. As enacted in 2016, B.01.008.3(2)(a) read "in descending order of the proportion of all the sugars-based ingredients in the prepackaged product or as a percentage of the prepackaged product, and the order or percentage shall be…"; the "or as a percentage" option is gone from the current text (see (vi)).

CFIA explanation, verbatim (list-of-ingredients page, Date modified 2026-02-11): "Sugars-based ingredients are required to be grouped within the list of ingredients following the term "Sugars" [B.01.008.3(1), FDR]." "Sugars-based ingredients can be 1 of the following: Ingredient that is a monosaccharide or disaccharide or a combination of these … Therefore, a sugars-based ingredient that is a monosaccharide, disaccharide or a combination of these refers to sugars that have their common name ending in "-ose" or contains the word "sugar". This includes glucose-fructose, cane sugar, sucrose, beet sugar, and lactose." "Ingredient that is a sweetening agent — This refers to sweetening agents as defined in, but not limited to, volume 15 of the CFCS document… Examples of sweetening agents include fancy molasses, maple syrup, brown sugar, agave syrup, refined sugar syrup, honey, and other syrup." "Ingredient that is a functional substitute for a sweetening agent — … a food, that is not a sweetener or sweetening agent including any sugars, but replaces a sweetening agent and has 1 or more functions of the sweetening agent including, sweetening, thickening, texturing or caramelizing [B.01.001(1), FDR]". Order: "When a prepackaged product contains 1 or more sugars-based ingredients, these ingredients must be declared in parentheses immediately following the term "Sugars" in the list of ingredients [B.01.008.3(1), FDR]. The order must be in descending order of proportion by weight of all the sugars-based ingredients added to the mixing bowl [B.01.008.3(2), FDR]." CFIA's worked example: "Ingredients: Sugars (fancy molasses, brown sugar, sugar) • Flour • Butter • Liquid whole egg • Salt • Sodium bicarbonate • Spices • Allura Red". Another CFIA example with fruit-derived sugars: "Ingredients: Water • Sugars (concentrated white grape juice, strawberry puree, concentrated raspberry juice, concentrated blackberry juice) • White wine vinegar…" with the explanation that concentrated juices and strawberry puree "are functional substitutes for a sweetening agent and must be grouped together in brackets following the term "Sugars"". Exemption: "Sugar alcohols (for example, maltitol) and swe[eteners]…" are not grouped; also products with a single sugars-based ingredient whose name contains "sugar" (B.01.008.3(4)(d)).

**How to read "Sugars (glucose-fructose, sugar), strawberries, …":** Under B.01.008.3(2)(a) the word "Sugars" is placed in the list according to the *combined* weight of all sugars-based ingredients; therefore, when "Sugars (…)" precedes "strawberries", the combined weight of glucose-fructose plus sugar exceeds the weight of strawberries as added to the batch. Under B.01.008.3(3)(b)(i), inside the parentheses glucose-fructose precedes sugar because glucose-fructose was the heavier of the two. The rule does not disclose the individual weights or the fruit percentage. Conversely, a list such as "Sugar, Strawberries, …" (single sugars-based ingredient whose name contains "sugar", exempt under (4)(d)) is read under B.01.008.2(3)(a): sugar outweighs strawberries.

Real Canadian examples of the two formats (both retailer/company pages, (a), opened 2026-09-26):
- Bonne Maman "Strawberry Jam" (bonnemaman.ca, Canadian site): "Sugars (sugar, golden sugar), Strawberries, Concentrated lemon juice, Pectin." https://bonnemaman.ca/en/products/confiture-de-fraises
- Kraft Strawberry Jam 500 mL (Voilà by Sobeys product page): "Sugar, Strawberries, Citric Acid (Helps Pectin Set Jam), Fruit Pectin, Dimethylpolysiloxane (Prevents Foaming). (E013F)" https://voila.ca/products/268030EA/details (page carries the retailer disclaimer "Please always refer to the product package for the most current and accurate ingredient and nutrition information").

Compliance date for the sugars grouping: not verified this session (see UNVERIFIED).

### (iii) "Glucose-fructose" and high-fructose corn syrup

What the primary sources actually say:
- CFIA Annex 1A ("examples of sugars-based ingredients that are monosaccharide, disaccharide or a combination of these") lists, as separate entries, "Glucose-fructose" and "High-fructose corn syrup" (also "Fructose syrup", "High-maltose corn syrup"); CFIA text: "This includes glucose-fructose, cane sugar, sucrose, beet sugar, and lactose." (a) list-of-ingredients page above.
- CFCS Volume 2 interpretation: "sweetening agent means glucose-fructose, fructose syrup or any food for which a standard is set out in Volume 15, Sweetening Agents or any combination thereof." (a) CFCS page. No CFCS standard defines "glucose-fructose".
- CFIA nutrient-content page (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/nutrient-content/requirements (Date modified 2025-01-15) lists among representations that are not nutrient content claims "some established common names such as "defatted soybeans", "high fructose corn syrup" and "demineralized water" [B.01.502(2)(h), FDR]" — i.e., CFIA treats "high fructose corn syrup" as an established common name.
- Health Canada's Natural Health Products Ingredients Database entry "High fructose corn syrup" (id 577) lists proper names "Corn syrup, high fructose" / "High fructose corn syrup" and common name "High fructose corn syrup"; "glucose-fructose" does not appear. (a) https://webprod.hc-sc.gc.ca/nhpid-bdipsn/ingredReq?id=577
- **No Health Canada or CFIA page was found stating that "glucose-fructose" is the Canadian name for what the US calls high-fructose corn syrup.** Searches of canada.ca and inspection.canada.ca returned none. The only named-outlet statement found (b): The Globe and Mail, Carly Weeks, May 2, 2014, "Too much sweetness: 'All sugars aren't the same'": "In Canada, it has a different name: 'glucose-fructose' or 'sugar/glucose-fructose,' which causes many label readers to mistake it for regular sugar." https://www.theglobeandmail.com/life/health-and-fitness/health/too-much-sweetness-all-sugars-arent-the-same/article18406886/ (only the naming sentence is used here). The script should attribute the equivalence to the Globe and Mail, not to Health Canada/CFIA, unless a government source is later found.

### (iv) "No sugar added" / "unsweetened"

- FDR B.01.509 (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.509.html: "B.01.509 (1) A person may, on the label of or in any advertisement for a food, make the statement or claim that the food is "unsweetened" if (a) the food meets the conditions set out in item 40, column 2, of the Table of Permitted Nutrient Content Statements and Claims for the subject "No added sugars" set out in column 1; and (b) the food does not contain a sweetener. (2) Despite subsection (1), no person shall, on the principal display panel of a prepackaged product, make a statement or claim that the product is "unsweetened" if a nutrition symbol referring to sugars appears on the panel. SOR/2003-11, s. 20; SOR/2022-168, s. 19; SOR/2024-244, s. 21". (The conditions table is no longer "the table following B.01.513"; it is Health Canada's incorporated-by-reference "Table of Permitted Nutrient Content Statements and Claims".)
- Table of Permitted Nutrient Content Statements and Claims, item 40 (document version shown "September 2, 2025"; landing page Date modified 2025-07-11) (a) https://www.canada.ca/en/health-canada/services/technical-documents-labelling-requirements/table-permitted-nutrient-content-statements-claims/table-document.html — Column 1: "No added sugars". Column 2: "(1) The food contains no added sugars-based ingredients or ingredients containing sugars-based ingredients. (2) The sugars content is not increased through some other means except if the functional effect is not to increase the sugars content of the food. (3) The similar reference food (a) contains an added sugars-based ingredient or an ingredient containing a sugars-based ingredient; and (b) does not meet the conditions set out in column 2 of item 37.1". Column 3: "N/A". Column 4: ""no sugar added", "no added sugar" or "without added sugar"". Item 37 "Free of sugars", column 2: "The food (a) contains less than 0.5 g of sugars per reference amount and serving of stated size; and (b) with the exception of chewing gum, meets the conditions set out in column 2 of item 2"; column 4 includes "sugar-free", "free of sugar", "no sugar", "0 sugar", "zero sugar", "without sugar", "contains no sugar", "sugarless"…
- CFIA "Criteria for the nutrient content claim no added sugars" (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/nutrient-content/no-added-sugars (Date modified 2024-03-18): the food "contains no added sugars-based ingredients or ingredients containing sugars-based ingredients"; sugars-based ingredient = "an ingredient that is a monosaccharide or disaccharide or a combination of these" or "a sweetening agent" or "any other ingredient that contains 1 or more sugars and that is added to the product as a functional substitute for a sweetening agent"; "unsweetened" additionally requires that the food "does not contain a sweetener, such as aspartame, sucralose, acesulfame-potassium or permitted sugar alcohols". Implication for jam: a "no sugar added" fruit spread sweetened with fruit juice concentrate cannot make the claim, because juice concentrate used as a sweetener is a sugars-based ingredient (functional substitute); this is a reading of the regulatory text, not a CFIA statement about any brand.

### (v) Sweetener (e.g., sucralose) labelling — what changed

- Pre-2022 FDR B.01.016 (version "from 2019-01-15 to 2022-07-19") (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.016-20190115.html: "B.01.016 The label of a food, other than a table-top sweetener, that contains sucralose shall carry the following information: (a) subject to paragraph (b), a statement on the principal display panel to the effect that the food contains sucralose or is sweetened with sucralose, in letters of at least the same size and prominence as the letters used for showing the numerical quantity in the declaration of net quantity…; (b) where sucralose is used in conjunction with another sweetener or a sweetening agent or both, the names thereof shown in a statement on the principal display panel…; and (c) a statement setting out the sucralose content expressed in milligrams per serving of stated size." (Parallel B.01.014 for aspartame required a principal-display-panel statement, a phenylalanine statement and mg per serving.)
- SOR/2022-168 repealed B.01.015–B.01.023 effective 2022-07-20: Justice Laws shows "B.01.015 [Repealed, SOR/2022-168, s. 9]" (version from 2022-07-20) (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.015-20220720.html, and B.01.014 was rewritten (version 2022-07-20 to 2024-12-17) to require only the phenylalanine statement for aspartame "immediately after the list of ingredients". (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.014-20220720.html
- Health Canada, "Changes to sweetener information on food labels" (Date modified 2023-03-03) (a) https://www.canada.ca/en/health-canada/services/food-nutrition/food-safety/food-additives/sugar-substitutes/changes-information-sweeteners.html: "These sweeteners don't have to be listed on the front of the package anymore. The amount of the sweetener in mg per serving will no longer appear in the list of ingredients." "All sweeteners used in food will still appear in the list of ingredients on the product label." "Regulated parties have until January 1, 2026 to meet the new requirements." "foods sweetened with aspartame must still include a statement warning individuals with phenylketonuria that the food contains phenylalanine. This statement must now appear in bold at the end of the list of ingredients."
- SOR/2024-244 s. 12: "Sections B.01.014 to B.01.023 of the Regulations are repealed." and the aspartame statement re-enacted as "B.16.016 (1) The label of a food that contains aspartame must carry a statement warning individuals with phenylketonuria that the food contains phenylalanine…" (a) Gazette URL above; Justice Laws now shows "B.01.014 [Repealed, SOR/2024-244, s. 12]".
- CFIA sweeteners page (Date modified 2025-01-15) (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/sweeteners: "Like all food additives, sweeteners must be declared in the list of ingredients of the prepackaged foods which they are present in [B.01.008(1)(b), FDR]." Result for jam: sucralose in a "no sugar added" fruit spread need only appear in the ingredient list; no principal-display-panel "sweetened with sucralose" statement and no mg/serving declaration is required (transition ended January 1, 2026 per Health Canada). Note the task's "2016 amendments" framing is wrong: the PDP repeal came via SOR/2022-168 (2022), consolidated by SOR/2024-244.

### (vi) Is fruit percentage mandatory on a jam label? No.

- No provision of the FDR or SFCR requires a jam label to state the percentage of fruit. The compositional standard (CFCS 10.4.1) sets a minimum the food must meet; B.01.042(a)-(d) requires compliance with the standard but adds no label declaration of fruit content.
- The FDR's general option to list ingredients "as a percentage" was removed: B.01.008.2(3)(a) as enacted by SOR/2016-305 read "(a) in descending order of their proportion of the prepackaged product or as a percentage of the prepackaged product, and the order or percentage shall be the order or percentage of the ingredients before they are combined to form the product;" (a) Gazette 2016 URL; archived version "from 2021-03-31 to 2022-06-20" still carries that wording (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.008.2-20210331.html; the version "from 2022-06-21 to 2022-07-19" (amendment history ending "SOR/2022-143, s. 6") reads only "in descending order of their proportion by weight of the prepackaged product, determined before they are combined to form the prepackaged product;" (a) https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.01.008.2-20220621.html. So since 2022-06-21 percentages are not part of the ingredient-list format.
- Voluntary emphasis triggers an expectation of quantity: CFIA composition-and-quality page (a): "In principle, any emphasis regarding the presence of an ingredient, component or substance should be accompanied by a statement regarding the amount of that ingredient, component or substance present in the food." and "It is misleading to over-emphasize the importance, presence or absence of an ingredient or substance in a food because of its desirable or undesirable qualities, or for any other reason." (Under SFCA s. 6(1) and SFCR 199(1)(b).) CFIA origin page gives the model "10% Italian olive oil" as a permissible highlighted-ingredient statement.

## 4. Origin claims

Source (a): CFIA "Origin claims on food labels", https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/origin-claims — Date modified 2023-12-06 (the task's URL "origin-claims-food-labels" returns HTTP 410 Gone).

- "Product of Canada" threshold: "The claim "Canadian" is considered to be the same as a "Product of Canada" claim. As such, all or virtually all major ingredients, processing, and labour used to make the food product must be Canadian." "…the percentage referred to as very little or minor is considered to be less than a total of 2% of the product". Example: "For example, a cookie that is manufactured in Canada from oatmeal, enriched flour, butter, honey and milk from Canada, and imported vanilla, may use the claim "Product of Canada" even if the vitamins in the flour and the vanilla are not from Canada." Also: "Generally, products that are exported and re-imported into Canada would not be able to make a "Product of Canada" claim."
- CFIA FAQ (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/origin-claims/frequently-asked-questions: ""All or virtually all" means that all the significant ingredients in a food product are Canadian in origin and that non-Canadian material is negligible in the food. Ingredients in a food that are present at very low levels and that are not generally produced in Canada, including spices, food additives, vitamins, minerals, and flavouring preparations, may be used without disqualifying the food from making [the claim]". (CFIA expresses the rule as "less than a total of 2%" non-Canadian content — i.e., the "98%" figure in the task is CFIA's rule stated the other way round; the literal "98%" wording appears in law-firm summaries (c), not on the CFIA page.)
- "Made in Canada": "A "Made in Canada" claim with a qualifying statement can be used on a food product when the last substantial transformation of the product occurred in Canada, even if some ingredients are from other countries." "A substantial transformation occurs when a food product undergoes processing which changes its nature and becomes a new product bearing a new name commonly understood by the consumer." "If the "Made in Canada" claim is used, it must also include a qualifying statement to indicate that the food product is made in Canada from imported ingredients or a combination of imported and domestic ingredients. The qualifying statements that can be used include "Made in Canada from domestic and imported ingredients" or "Made in Canada from imported ingredients"." "All variations of "Made in Canada" claims must include a qualifying statement." "When a food is made with ingredients that are all sourced from outside of Canada, the label would state "Made in Canada from imported ingredients"."
- Other domestic-content claims (same page): ""Processed in Canada" to describe a food which has been entirely processed in Canada"; ""Prepared in Canada" to describe a food which has been entirely prepared in Canada"; ""Packaged in Canada" to describe a food which is imported in bulk and packaged in Canada"; ""Refined in Canada" to describe imported cane sugar which has been refined in Canada".
- Multiple-country statements: "The use of a voluntary multi-country of origin statement (for example, "Product of France and United States") is not acceptable. In this context, a product can only have one country of origin, which, at a minimum, is the country of last substantial transformation."
- Imported jam must show it is imported. CFIA "Name and principal place of business" (Date modified 2022-07-06) (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/name-and-principal-place-business: "Consumer prepackaged foods wholly manufactured, processed or produced outside of Canada must indicate "Imported by" / "importé par" or "Imported for" / "importé pour" as part of the name and principal place of business declaration, unless the geographic origin of the foods is shown on the label. This requirement applies whether the consumer prepackaged foods are repackaged, labelled or re-labelled in Canada or elsewhere [223(1), SFCR]." CFIA "Country of origin" (Date modified 2022-07-19) (a) https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/country-origin: "When a food product is wholly manufactured outside of Canada, the label must show that the product is imported." and "it is mandatory to state the country of origin on some specific imported prepackaged products… processed fruit and vegetable products … Generally, for the above foods, the words "Product of [Name the country of origin]" must appear on the label." Jam is a processed fruit product (CFIA processed-fruit page: "Examples of processed fruit or vegetable products include: … jam, jelly, marmalade and pie filling").

Label examples found (a): Kraft Heinz Away From Home Canada product page for "Kraft PURE Strawberry Jam with Pectin (200 x 16 mL Cups)": "Kraft PURE Jams with Pectin are Proudly Prepared in Canada" https://www.kraftheinzawayfromhome.com/en-CA/products/00068100897838-kraft-pure-strawberry-jam-with-pectin-200-x-16-m-l-cups. Costco Business Centre Canada, "Kirkland Signature Organic Strawberry Spread, 2 x 550 mL" (item 1975373): "Made in Canada from Imported Ingredients" https://www.costcobusinesscentre.ca/p/-/kirkland-signature-organic-strawberry-spread-2-x-550-ml/2001216171. Greaves: "All of our products are proudly prepared in Niagara-on-the-Lake, Canada" https://greavesjams.com/company/.

## 5. Legal and enforcement record

### 5.1 CFIA recalls involving jam/jelly/spreads (recalls-rappels.canada.ca, food category; searches "jam", "jams", "jelly", "marmalade", "confiture", "preserves", "spread", "strawberry jam", "raspberry jam", "apricot jam", "fig jam" run 2026-09-26). All are (a).

Within the last five years (Sept 2021 – Sept 2026):
- **2026-08-12** (recall date 2026-08-07): "Alfa brand croissant products recalled due to mould" — includes "Croissant Double with Strawberry Jam & Vanilla Cream Filling" (4 x 6 x 75 g); recalling firm MFG Distribution Inc.; distribution National; reason "Food - Microbial contamination - Non harmful (quality or spoilage)"; Class 3; no illnesses mentioned. https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/alfa-brand-croissant-products-recalled-due-mould (The only "confiture" hit on the French site is this same recall.)
- **2026-03-16** (recall date 2026-03-12): "My Oma Always... brand Red Pepper Jelly recalled due to possible pieces of glass" — 250 ml, UPC 6 28942 72938 6, code BB 28 FE 19; recalling firm Prairie Hill Farms Ltd.; Alberta and British Columbia; Class 3; no illnesses mentioned. https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/my-oma-always-brand-red-pepper-jelly-recalled-due-possible-pieces-glass
- **2024-12-10/13**: "Certain bakery products recalled due to pieces of metal" — Bonchaz Bakery, British Columbia, Class 2; affected items include "SB Jam 6-pack (10 x 265 g)", "SB Apricot Singles", "SB Raspberry Singles"; cause "affected salt was used to make these recalled products" (linked to the Sifto Hy·Grade Salt recall). https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/certain-bakery-products-recalled-due-pieces-metal
- No recall of a retail jar of jam for **undeclared sulphites** was found in any of the searches; the only sulphite hit near "strawberry" was a cider (Solstice Ciderworks Strawberry Rhubarb Cider, 2025-08-06), not jam.

Just outside the five-year window (for context):
- 2021-04-27 (updated 2021-05-03): "Various brands of Fig Spread recalled due to presence of stones" — Earl's Fig Spread 5 kg, Recipe Fig Spread 1.5 L, Woolwich Fig Spread 23 kg, Woolwich Fig Spread with Balsamic Vinegar 23 kg; recalling firm W.T. Lynch Foods Limited; AB, BC, NB, ON, SK, YT; Class 3. https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/various-brands-fig-spread-recalled-due-presence-stones
- 2021-02-01 (updated 2021-02-03): "ST. DALFOUR brand Deluxe Chestnut Spread recalled due to pieces of glass" — 225 ml, UPC 0 8438098561 4, code 2022/JA/16; recalling firm Satau Inc.; Ontario and Quebec (retail); Class 2. https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/st-dalfour-brand-deluxe-chestnut-spread-recalled-due-pieces-glass (Note: the recalling firm is Satau Inc., not Husky Food Importers — see §6 St. Dalfour.)
- 2020-03-03 (updated 2020-03-10): "Tamek brand Sour Cherry Jam with Pectin recalled due to presence of cherry pits" — 320 ml, UPC 0 58504 72178 5; recalling firm AGT Clic Foods Inc.; BC, ON, QC; Class 3. https://recalls-rappels.canada.ca/en/alert-recall/tamek-brand-sour-cherry-jam-pectin-recalled-due-presence-cherry-pits

### 5.2 CFIA prosecutions / AMPs; Competition Bureau

- Searches of inspection.canada.ca enforcement pages (AMP quarterly lists, "Enforcement actions taken") for jam / fruit spread / confiture returned no jam-specific notice of violation, AMP or prosecution. **Nothing found** (not proof of absence; the AMP lists were not read line by line).
- Competition Bureau: no case, consent agreement or settlement involving jam or fruit-spread labelling was found on competition-bureau.canada.ca ("Deceptive marketing practices—Cases and outcomes"). **Nothing found.** For food, "Product of Canada"/"Made in Canada" claims are enforced by CFIA under the FDA and SFCA (CFIA origin page).

### 5.3 Class actions — United States (court dockets via CourtListener/RECAP, (a); opened 2026-09-26)

1. **Ringler v. The J.M. Smucker Company**, No. 2:25-cv-01138, U.S. District Court, C.D. California (Judge Anne Hwang). Filed 2025-02-10. **Allegation** (complaint; (d) copy hosted by truthinadvertising.org https://truthinadvertising.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Ringler-v-Smuckers-complaint.pdf): Smucker's "Natural" fruit spreads labelled "natural"/"made with ingredients from natural sources" while containing citric acid alleged to be artificially produced. **Outcome:** docket entry 99 (2026-04-29) "STIPULATION to Dismiss Case pursuant to Fed. R. Civ. P. 41(a)(1)(A)(ii) filed by Plaintiffs Jana Rabinowitz, Adina Ringler, Krista Robles, Jay Smith"; entry 100 (2026-05-04) "In light of the Stipulation of dismissal 99, the Court hereby closes this case… Case Terminated." A class-certification motion (Dkt 86) was pending at the time. **No finding, no admission; whether a settlement underlies the stipulation is not on the docket.** https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69627546/adina-ringler-v-the-jm-smucker-company/
2. **Francy Graham v. Andros Foods USA, Inc.**, No. 2:25-cv-03561, C.D. California (Magistrate Judge A. Joel Richlin). Filed 2025-04-22. **Allegation** (b: Bloomberg Law, April 23, 2025, https://news.bloomberglaw.com/litigation/bonne-maman-maker-faces-made-in-france-consumer-deception-suit): Bonne Maman labelling/marketing suggested the preserves and jellies were "exclusively" made in France while some were made in Belgium. **Finding:** amended complaint 2025-10-30; motion to dismiss filed 2025-11-13, heard 2026-01-07; entry 54 (2026-05-26) "JUDGMENT … IT IS HEREBY ADJUDGED that this action is DISMISSED WITH PREJUDICE… Related to: Order on Motion to Dismiss Case, 53"; entry 55 (2026-06-24) "Appeal to 9th Circuit Court of Appeals"; entry 56 (2026-06-30) "USCA Notification of Case Number". **Dismissed for the defendant; appeal pending. No admission.** https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69927763/francy-graham-v-andros-foods-usa-inc/
3. **J.M. Smucker Company v. Andros Foods USA, Inc. / Andros S.N.C.**, No. 5:11-cv-01544, N.D. Ohio (Judge John R. Adams). Filed 2011-07-26. Nature: Smucker sought a declaratory judgment in a trade-dress dispute over gingham-pattern jar lids after Andros's cease-and-desist (b: Adweek, "Jam War Spreads to Federal Court", https://www.adweek.com/brand-marketing/jam-war-spreads-federal-court-133976/). **Outcome:** entry 62 (2013-03-06) "Proposed Stipulation of Dismissal with Prejudice filed by J.M. Smucker Company"; entry 63 (2013-03-07) "Order dismissing this matter with prejudice. Each party to bear their own costs and attorney's fees." **No finding on the merits; no admission; terms not on docket.** https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/13000202/jm-smucker-company-v-andros-foods-usa-inc/
4. **Crosson v. Andros Foods USA, Inc.**, No. 1:22-cv-02528, E.D. New York — an Americans with Disabilities Act (website accessibility) case, not a labelling case; voluntarily dismissed 2022-07-01. Listed only so the script does not mistake it for a jam-labelling suit. https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/63283633/crosson-v-andros-foods-usa-inc/
5. **Park et al. v. Welch Foods Inc.**, No. 3:12-cv-06449, N.D. California. Filed 2012-12-20. **Allegation** (d: TINA.org summary https://truthinadvertising.org/class-action/welchs-100-juices-natural-spreads/): "the company misleadingly labels Natural Spreads as 'all natural' when they actually contain artificial flavors, colors, and preservatives." Docket: administrative closure 2018-08-16 after mediation; entry 100 (2019-04-15) "Joint MOTION to Dismiss Case with Prejudice filed by Welch Foods Inc."; entry 101 (2019-04-16) "ORDER by Judge Thomas S. Hixson granting 100 Motion to Dismiss with Prejudice." **Joint dismissal with prejudice; no finding; no admission; terms not on docket.** (TINA.org's note that a dismissal motion was denied in March 2014 and the case stayed in October 2015 is from a (d) source; the March 2014 order was not read.) https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/4179777/park-v-welch-foods-inc/
6. **Smucker "Simply 100% Fruit" litigation (2003–2004).** Company's own filing (a): J.M. Smucker Co. Form 10-K for fiscal year ended April 30, 2004, Item 3: "The Company is a defendant in 18 class action lawsuits in ten states related to its Simply 100% Fruit product. The Company also has two class action lawsuits in two states related to its Dickinson 100% Fruit product. The complaints in these lawsuits generally allege violations of state consumer fraud acts, unjust enrichment and breach of an express warranty based on the allegation that Simply 100% Fruit does not contain 100 percent fruit and it does not contain 100 percent of the fruit designated as the flavor (e.g., strawberry). The complaints generally seek damages in the form of either a refund of the purchase price or the difference between the price of Simply 100% Fruit and lower priced Smucker products. The Company believes these suits are without merit and intends to vigorously defend these actions." https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/91419/000095015204005304/l08405ae10vk.htm. **Allegation only; company denial.** The FY2005 10-K (filed 2005-07-12) and FY2006 10-K Item 3 no longer mention these suits (they discuss a California environmental matter only) — the disposition is therefore undocumented in SEC filings (see UNVERIFIED). Background record (d): Center for Science in the Public Interest press statement, May 13, 2003, https://www.cspi.org/new/200305131.html — CSPI said its tests found the strawberry "Simply 100% Fruit" contained about 30% strawberries and the blueberry about 43% blueberries, and called on FDA to act; quote: "Calling these spreads '100 percent fruit' is 100 percent fraudulent." (advocacy-group allegation; no regulator finding located). Trade coverage: Progressive Grocer, "Smucker Sued Over '100 Percent Fruit'" https://progressivegrocer.com/smucker-sued-over-100-percent-fruit (b; page 403 on 2026-09-26, not read).

No US or Canadian class action was found naming E.D. Smith, Crofter's, St. Dalfour or Winland Foods over jam labelling. Welch's other suits found (fruit snacks slack-fill, "no preservatives", fibre, juice) concern products other than jam/jelly and are not included.

### 5.4 Canada — class actions

None found against Smucker's, Bonne Maman/Meilleures Marques, Andros, E.D. Smith/Winland, Kraft Heinz Canada, Welch's, St. Dalfour or Crofter's over jam labelling (searches of Canadian class-action registries and general web). **Nothing found.**

No DGCCRF sanction, Foodwatch or UFC-Que Choisir action concerning Bonne Maman/Andros jam composition in 2024 was found. The only French government record found was a consumer recall notice for "CONFITURES de fraises en mini pots verre de 30 g BONNE MAMAN" on rappel.conso.gouv.fr (fiche 23072) — details not read (see UNVERIFIED). **Nothing usable.**

## 7. Quick reference — what the script may state as fact (with source tier)

- Canada's legal minimum for "(fruit) Jam": 45% named fruit, 66% water-soluble solids; "Jam with Pectin": 27% / 66%; "Jelly": 62% solids; "Marmalade": 65% solids (no fruit minimum); "Preserve/Conserve": 45:55 fruit:sweetener (dry basis), 60% solids — CFCS Vol. 10 s. 10.4, incorporated by reference in the FDR since 2024-12-18; identical numbers in FDR B.11.201–B.11.241 before that. (a)
- "Fruit spread" has no standard in Canada; a product called "fruit spread" is one that is not jam, jelly or marmalade (Smucker Foods of Canada FAQ). (a)
- "Preserves" is a separate Canadian standard, not a synonym for jam. (a)
- Ingredients are listed by descending weight before mixing; all sugars-based ingredients are grouped after "Sugars" and positioned by their combined weight (FDR B.01.008.2(3)(a), B.01.008.3). (a)
- Fruit percentage need not be declared; percentages left the ingredient-list format on 2022-06-21. (a)
- Sucralose/aspartame no longer need a front-of-pack statement or mg/serving (SOR/2022-168; Health Canada compliance date January 1, 2026). (a)
- "Product of Canada" = all or virtually all Canadian (non-Canadian content under 2%); "Made in Canada" needs the qualifier "from domestic and imported ingredients" or "from imported ingredients"; imported jam must carry "Imported by/for" or country of origin. (a)
- Ringler v. Smucker: dismissed by stipulation 2026-05-04, no admission. Graham v. Andros (Bonne Maman "Made in France"): dismissed with prejudice 2026-05-26, on appeal. Park v. Welch (Natural Spreads): joint dismissal with prejudice 2019-04-16. Smucker v. Andros (2011 lid dispute): stipulated dismissal 2013-03-07. (a)

---

## UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. **"Glucose-fructose is what the US calls high-fructose corn syrup" as a Health Canada/CFIA statement** — no government page found; only the Globe and Mail (2014) says it. Attribute to the Globe or omit.
2. **Compliance/enforcement date for the sugars-grouping rule (December 14, 2021 / 2022 extension)** — not verified; only the 2016-12-14 Gazette registration is confirmed.
3. **Whether pectin, Class II preservatives, pH-adjusting and antifoaming agents remain permitted in plain "Jam" after the standard text moved to the CFCS** — the CFCS 10.4.1 text omits them; Health Canada's Lists of Permitted Food Additives were not checked.
4. **Kraft Heinz discontinuing Kraft jam in Canada** — no company statement or named-outlet report found; the only material is consumer forum posts (c). Kraft Pure jam still appears on Metro/Voilà/Food Basics listings.
5. **Where E.D. Smith jams are made today (Winona, Ontario) and the plant's headcount ("240")** — edsmith.com was unreachable (HTTP 503 / connection failure); the only sources found are Wikipedia and madeinca.ca (c). The Globe (2007) confirms Winona as a plant only as of 2007.
6. **The exact "Imported by Smucker Foods of Canada Corp." / "Product of USA" wording on Canadian Smucker's jars** — inferred from the 10-K plant list, not read from a label.
7. **Bonne Maman "#2 brand in Canada's Jams, Jellies and Marmalades category… Nielsen… week ending 06/20/20"** — appears in a 2020 BusinessWire release snippet; businesswire.com returned 403; not read verbatim.
8. **Bonne Maman Metro listing ingredient order ("Strawberries, Sugar, Golden Sugar…")** — search snippet only (page 403); it conflicts with the company page's "Sugars (sugar, golden sugar), Strawberries…". Use the company page.
9. **Outcome of the 2003–2004 "Simply 100% Fruit" class actions (Smith v. J.M. Smucker, Cook County No. 03CH08522, and 17 others)** — not documented in later 10-Ks; unknown whether dismissed, settled or relabelled.
10. **Any 2024 French/EU regulatory matter involving Bonne Maman/Andros jam** — nothing found; the rappel.conso.gouv.fr strawberry mini-pot recall (fiche 23072) was seen only as a search snippet.
11. **Chatelaine's strawberry-jam ranking** — page blocked; content unknown.
12. **CBC Marketplace testing jam fruit content** — no such story found.
13. **Kirkland Signature Organic Strawberry Spread (Canada): manufacturer and full ingredient list** — "Made in Canada from Imported Ingredients" is confirmed; the maker (Crofter's is speculated online) is not; the ingredient list seen ("fresh organic strawberries, organic cane sugar, fruit pectin, citric acid") came from a search snippet of costco.ca, not the rendered page.
14. **Welch's Canadian importer/distributor and origin statement** — not found.
15. **Podravka/S&F Canadian importer; Podravka jam at Food Basics** — not found; S&F "Product of Croatia" confirmed only via a Calgary retailer's page.
16. **Current legal entity and ownership of Pied-Mont Dora (Dora jams)** — only the company's own history narrative ("Limoges family", "Conserves des Laurentides") was read; no registry check.
17. **St. Dalfour's current Canadian importer** — Husky Food Importers lists the brand; the 2021 CFIA recall names Satau Inc.; the label's "Imported by" was not read. Husky's "Loire Valley" wording conflicts with the brand's "Marmande".
18. **"98%" as CFIA's Product of Canada threshold** — CFIA's own wording is "less than a total of 2%" non-Canadian content and "all or virtually all"; the "98%" figure literally appears only in law-firm and Wikipedia summaries (c).
19. **Any CFIA AMP or prosecution involving jam** — none found, but the quarterly AMP tables were not read line by line.
20. **Progressive Grocer (2003) and CBC (2007 E.D. Smith; 2020 pumpkin-pie-jam recall) articles** — returned 403; contents not read; not quoted above.
21. **Habitant-brand jams being made by E.D. Smith in Winona** — (c) only; note Smucker's 10-K shows the Habitant *pickled beets* trademark went to TreeHouse in 2024.
22. **Canadian Standards of Identity Vol. 4 archived (pre-2024) text** — not read; the current version (Date modified 2024-12-18) is quoted.

# TASK P2 — Canadian shelf price, label and ingredient grid for jam, plus popularity proxy and price series

Compiled 26 September 2026. Every URL below was opened on 26 September 2026 unless stated. Tier codes: (a) primary — regulator, court, StatCan, company's own page/filing, retailer's own product page, flyer or feed; (a*) retailer's own flyer/feed reached through the Flipp API (retailer-authored content, aggregator transport); (b) named outlet; (c) aggregator/blog/SEO/AI summary — NOT USED as a source, only flagged; (d) documented consumer/media record. Every price in this file: **re-verify on shelf on camera**. No health, nutrition or sweetener commentary is made anywhere in this file; where a label carries a Health Canada front-of-package nutrition symbol, that is recorded only as a label fact.

Scope note on stores: the brief says "eleven Loblaw stores" but lists ten store IDs (Loblaws 1032 Toronto, 7155 Vancouver; Real Canadian Superstore 1539 Calgary, 1517 Vancouver; No Frills 7952 Toronto, 3155 Calgary, 3410 Vancouver; Atlantic Superstore 0354 Halifax; Provigo 7297 Montréal; Maxi 9528 Montréal). ## 1. Regulatory frame for the "legal common name" column (all tier a)

**1.1 Food and Drug Regulations, C.R.C., c. 870, as amended by SOR/2024-244.**
- B.11.201 (current text, opened 26 Sep 2026, https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.11.201.html): "B.11.201 No person shall sell fruit jam or jam containing rhubarb unless the jam has been obtained by boiling the fruit or rhubarb, as the case may be, with water and a sweetening ingredient. SOR/92-400, s. 16; SOR/2024-244, s. 72."
- B.11.202 (formerly "Jam with Pectin") and B.11.203: "[Repealed, SOR/2024-244, s. 72]" (https://laws-lois.justice.gc.ca/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._870/section-B.11.202.html and …/section-B.11.203.html).
- B.11.240 (jelly): "No person shall sell fruit jelly unless it has been obtained by boiling the fruit, the juice of the fruit or a concentrate of the juice of the fruit with water and a sweetening ingredient. SOR/2024-244, s. 74." B.11.241, B.11.242, B.11.250: "[Repealed, SOR/2024-244, s. 74]".
- B.11.220 (citrus marmalade) and B.11.221 (pineapple/fig marmalade) likewise reduced to a boiling-with-sweetener definition (SOR/2024-244, s. 73).
- Canada Gazette, Part II, Vol. 158, No. 26, 18 December 2024, SOR/2024-244 "Regulations Amending Certain Regulations Concerning Food Additives and Compositional Standards, Microbiological Criteria and Methods of Analysis for Food", https://gazette.gc.ca/rp-pr/p2/2024/2024-12-18/html/sor-dors244-eng.html — s. 72: "Sections B.11.201 to B.11.203 of the Regulations are replaced by the following: B.11.201 No person shall sell fruit jam or jam containing rhubarb unless the jam has been obtained by boiling the fruit or rhubarb, as the case may be, with water and a sweetening ingredient." Registration: "November 29, 2024".

**1.2 Where the fruit-percentage standards now live: CFIA "Canadian Food Compositional Standards" (document incorporated by reference), Volume 10 – Fruits, Vegetables, Their Products and Substitutes, s. 10.4** (page "Date modified: 2025-05-01", https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/canadian-food-compositional-standards-0):
- "10.4.1 (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam is the product obtained by processing fruit, fruit pulp, or canned fruit, by boiling to a suitable consistency with water and a sweetening ingredient; (a) contains not less than (i) 45% of the named fruit, and (ii) 66% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer; (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit; and (c) does not contain apple or rhubarb."
- "10.4.2 (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam with Pectin is the product obtained by processing fruit, fruit pulp, or canned fruit by boiling to a suitable consistency with water and a sweetening ingredient; (a) contains (i) not less than 27% of the named fruit, (ii) not less than 66% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer, and (iii) pectin or pectinous preparations; (b) may contain such amount of acid ingredient as reasonably compensates for any deficiency in the natural acidity of the named fruit; and (c) does not contain apple or rhubarb."
- "10.4.8 (Naming the fruit or fruits) Preserve (Conserve) is the product made by processing fruit other than apple or rhubarb with a sweetening ingredient, and contains not less than (a) 45 parts by weight of the named fruit for each 55 parts by weight, on the dry basis, of a sweetening ingredient; and (b) 60% water-soluble solids, as estimated by the refractometer."
- "10.4.9 (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jelly is the gelatinous product, free of seeds and pulp, made from the named fruit, the juice of the named fruit or a concentrate of the juice of the named fruit, which has been boiled with water and a sweetening ingredient; (a) contains not less than 62% water soluble solids as estimated by the refractometer …" and "10.4.10 … Jelly with Pectin … (i) not less than the equivalent of 32% juice of the named fruit, (ii) not less than 62% water soluble solids … and (iii) pectin or pectinous preparation".
- CFIA "Canadian Standards of Identity: Volume 4 – Processed Fruit or Vegetable Products" (incorporated by reference in the Safe Food for Canadians Regulations; page "Date modified: 2024-12-18", https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/canadian-standards-identity-volume-4): "74. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.1 of Volume 10 of the FCSD, except that it (a) must not be obtained by processing fruit pulp that is preserved in sulphur dioxide; and (b) may contain a sweetening ingredient and concentrated juice. 75. (Naming the fruit or fruits) Jam with Pectin is the food that meets the standard set out in section 10.4.2 of Volume 10 of the FCSD except that it may contain a sweetening ingredient."
- Practical reading for the script: "Jam" and "Jam with pectin" are standardized common names (45% / 27% named fruit, 66% soluble solids). "Fruit spread", "Spread", "Jam-type spread", "Fruit and concentrated grape juice spread" are NOT standardized names, so no minimum fruit content attaches to them. Do not go further than this in the script; the file makes no claim about what any individual product's fruit content actually is.

**1.3 Front-of-package (FOP) nutrition symbol — why some 2026 jars carry a "High in / Élevé en · Sugars / Sucres · Health Canada / Santé Canada" box.** Health Canada, "Front-of-package nutrition symbol labelling guide for industry", https://www.canada.ca/en/health-canada/services/food-nutrition/legislation-guidelines/guidance-documents/front-package-nutrition-symbol-labelling-industry.html (opened 26 Sep 2026): "For most prepackaged products, a symbol must appear on the label when the amount of saturated fat, sugars and/or sodium is ≥ (equal to or greater than) 15% of the applicable DV"; "For prepackaged products with a reference amount ≤ (equal to or less than) 30 g or 30 mL, the symbol must appear when the amount of saturated fat, sugars and/or sodium is ≥ 10% of the applicable DV"; "Regulated parties must comply with the requirements for all components of these amendments as of January 1, 2026." Health Canada "Regulations and compliance – front-of-package nutrition labelling", https://www.canada.ca/en/health-canada/services/food-nutrition/food-labelling/nutrition-labelling/regulations-compliance/front-package-nutrition-labelling.html: regulations published in Canada Gazette, Part II on "July 20, 2022"; compliance date "January 1, 2026". (Jam's reference amount is 15 mL, so the 10% DV rule is the one that applies; that is a labelling fact, nothing more is asserted.)

## 2. PRODUCT GRID — one block per product

Format: **Product** — size as printed · legal common name as printed on label · front-label claims · ingredient declaration verbatim (source) · grouped "Sugars (…)" format? · fruit-% statement · origin statement · FOP symbol on label image? · prices (store, date) · $/100 mL or g · URLs/tier · discrepancies.

Loblaw price notation: REGULAR = shelf price with no promotion; "sale to YYYY-MM-DD" = `SPECIAL` price with expiry from the API; "[$5.50 MIN 2]" = multi-buy deal badge; per-100 uses the lowest regular price seen across the 10 stores. Full store-by-store grid is in §3.

Sugar-first; grouped: yes. **Habitant Jam Light Strawberry 500 mL** (168313EA): $4.79; "Strawberries, Sugar, Water, Fruit Pectin, Citric Acid, Concentrated Black Carrot Juice (Colour), Natural Flavour." The edsmith.com URL `https://edsmith.com/edsmith_product/strawberry-jam-pectin/` is titled "HABITANT® Strawberry Jam with Pectin" in search results, but edsmith.com could not be opened today (see §14) — Habitant's link to E.D. Smith is therefore **unverified** here, and Habitant is not returned at Loblaw (Habitant search at all five stores returns only soups).
- Ownership: TreeHouse Foods press release 3 Oct 2022 (https://www.treehousefoods.com/news-and-media/press-release-details/2022/TREEHOUSE-FOODS-COMPLETES-SALE-OF-SIGNIFICANT-PORTION-OF-ITS-MEAL-PREPARATION-BUSINESS-TO-INVESTINDUSTRIAL/default.aspx, opened 26 Sep 2026): sale to Investindustrial for $950 million; "The divested business has been renamed Winland Foods, Inc." The press release does **not** name E.D. Smith; that E.D. Smith/Winona was part of the divestiture is stated only by (c)-tier pages today — unverified.

- 250 mL (https://greavesjams.com/product/greaves-strawberry-jam-250ml/): **$9.25**; "Ingredients: 100% Pure Cane Sugar, Strawberries, Citric Acid"; "our #1 bestseller"; "Rated The Best Strawberry Jam in Canada!" (company's own claim). → $3.70/100 mL. 500 mL (…/greaves-strawberry-jam-500ml/): **$17.95** → $3.59/100 mL; "Our number one best seller, and for good reason." Home page: "Established 1927", "100% Canadian", 55 Queen Street. Sugar-first declaration; no pectin listed. Not found at any Loblaw store, flyer, Voilà or Walmart feed — direct/specialty only. Common name on jar not captured (site sells as "Strawberry Jam").

The label is the record; do not read Voilà's order on camera.

7. **Named colours vs "Colour":** Loblaw text for Smucker's Double Fruit/NSA/Double Fruit 500 names "Allura Red, Sunset Yellow FCF / Brilliant Blue FCF"; Voilà shows the generic word "Colour". Read the jar.
8. **Grape juice wording — Smucker's NSA:** Loblaw "Concentrated Grape Juice"; Voilà and smuckers.ca "White Grape Juice Concentrate / Concentrated White Grape Juice".
9. **Flyer wording vs label:** IGA "DOUBLE FRUIT JAM" (label: Fruit Spread); Loblaws "PIERRE BONNE FRUIT JAM" (raspberry jar: Raspberry Spread); Nesters "Crofter's Organic Jam" (label: Premium Spread); Sobeys "Jam - Big Earn" for St. Dalfour (label: Fruit Spread); Thrifty "Smucker's Pure Jam 250 ml" (current 250 mL label: Strawberry Spread).

11. **Bonne Maman image vintage:** Loblaw 250 mL image has no FOP box; 500 mL image (marked "NOUVEAU FORMAT – NEW SIZE") has the French-first box. Check which version is on shelf.

Badges found: Prepared in Canada — PC (all lines), no name, PC Organics, PC Black Label, Crofter's, Last Mountain, Natur, Healthy Crunch; Prepared in Québec — Dora (Maxi). No origin badge for Smucker's, Bonne Maman, St Dalfour, Veres, Galil, Streit's.

# TASK P3 — Popularity-ranking evidence, ownership and corporate-claim dossier: jam brands sold in Canada

Prepared 26 September 2026. Every URL below was opened on **26 Sep 2026** unless marked "NOT OPENED". Tiers: **(a)** primary (regulator, court, filing, company's own page/release, retailer's own product page), **(b)** named outlet, **(c)** aggregator/blog/SEO/Wikipedia/AI summary/market-research teaser — NOT USED as a source, only flagged, **(d)** documented consumer/media record. Scope: composition standards, legal common names, ingredient order, origin, ownership, price, legal record. No health/nutrition commentary. Where a corporate page carries a calorie claim it is noted as "calorie claim present — not reproduced (out of scope)".

Access notes affecting this dossier (26 Sep 2026): `edsmith.com` and `www.edsmith.com` returned HTTP 503 on every attempt (home, /our-story/, /contact-us/, /products/jams-jellies-fruit-spreads/, product pages) and a TLS handshake failure via curl; `winlandfoods.com` returned 503 / TLS failure; web.archive.org is blocked by egress policy; Walmart.ca, Metro.ca product pages, Voilà.ca, Chatelaine.com and PressReader served bot-challenge/403 pages. Those items are listed at the end as unverified.
